is a multi-instrumentalist, composer, performer and curator. We are talking about Taja Cheek aka L\u0026#x27;Rain. With her music, the New Yorker creates a world in which everything makes sense and is sensual, where duality and contradiction are omnipresent. Inspired by the gospel and R\u0026amp;B of the 1990s, from black folk and indie rock to the sounds of early synthesizers and Baroque compositional tropes, she creates songs that make use of certain styles but cannot be assigned to any genre. L\u0026#x27;Rain herself describes the practice and aesthetics of her music as \u0026quot;approaching songness\u0026quot; – approaching, touching and yet letting go again, which makes the diversity and ambivalence of her sonic explorations all the more powerful. “I’m envisioning a world of contradictions, as always,\u0026quot; explains Cheek. \u0026quot;Sensual, maybe even sexy, but terrifying and strange.\u0026quot; \u003cbr\u003e \u003cbr\u003eAt Currents, L\u0026#x27;Rain presents her third album \u0026quot;I killed your dog\u0026quot; together with her collaborators Andrew Lappin and Ben Chapoteau-Katz. is a journey. It begins in Algeria, in the working-class district of Hussein-Dey, where Nedjim Bouizzoul grows up surrounded by chaabi music. He emigrated to Quebec with his family and earned his living as a street musician and with small gigs in cafés. His first collaborations eventually led to the group Labess (which translates to “all is well”) and the debut album Tout va bien (2007). The music is open to all directions: African elements are combined with gypsy rumba and flamenco, always accompanied by chaabi. The Arabic, French and Spanish lyrics mix realism and hope, gentleness and anger. They tell of exile, of the joys and sorrows that accompany the journey from the homeland to the host country. A poetic reflection on cultural diversity and the need to unite everyone, regardless of our differences. Release «evolving»","text":"The newly formed 10-piece ensemble MetaLogue, led by singer Nina Reiter, is reviving the music of Lucerne composer Mani Planzer, who died in 1997 and was almost forgotten. As a musician, composer, organist, teacher, choirmaster and big band leader, Planzer had a decisive influence on the cultural landscape of Central Switzerland. He was awarded the Lucerne City Prize for Art and Culture in 1981 and the Lucerne Living Space Prize posthumously in 1998.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eThe band name \u0026quot;MetaLogue\u0026quot; refers to Planzer\u0026#x27;s penchant for wordplay and symbolises the symbiosis of the various elements that come together in this project. On the one hand, it is the young musicians who bring Planzer\u0026#x27;s music into the light of the present and allow the audience to participate in a musical dialogue that takes place far away from the mainstream. On the other hand, it is the lyrics of singer Nina Reiter, who picks up on Planzer\u0026#x27;s pioneering spirit in the synthesis of jazz and poetry, thus creating a meta-level to the previously instrumental compositions.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eOn the occasion of the upcoming anniversary – Planzer would have celebrated his 85th birthday in 2024 – the Mani Planzer Big Band and the
